- U-41 | stopped | Story 2.9: AD-26 amendment (re-architecture under U7c, so the owner's): AD-26's Rule counts a turn link's item index as 'the thread's headers up to the target plus every event-log, rule-log and I/O-log row logged up to the target's creation time'. Read from the IRIS source (not measured), Visual Trace draws less: an event-log or rule-log row appears only once its host is the source or target of an earlier header, and an I/O-log row is placed by its request, not by time. So the literal formula over-counts, and a late turn's link can open on a later page than the target, which is AD-26's own Prevents line ('the climax link opening on the wrong page') and 2.9's test 'a thread of more than 40 items opens Visual Trace on that turn'. AD-26 is cited by done specs (2.3, 2.4, 2.6, 2.7) and by Epic 1's in-flight specs (1.3-1.8). Epic 1 cites it only for trace field names and the trace target, not the item index, and no Epic 1 code computes the index. -> a, with one condition: the counting model is read from the IRIS source, not measured, so Story 2.9 (or 2.13) must prove it on the real viewer. A thread of more than 40 items, with a late turn after event-log, rule-log and I/O-log rows, is opened through the real Visual Trace page on a throwaway, events shown and hidden, and the test asserts that the target row is on the opened page. If the viewer and the model disagree, the measured behaviour wins and the AD-26 bullet is corrected to match (a veto item). The guarantees and the Prevents line stay as they are. (by Contest Planner, 2026-10-23T16:45:10Z#115; quoting the owner: "I have delegated authority to the Contest Planner. That agent is allowed to make decision on my behalf."); resolved by orchestrator, 2026-10-24T18:27:00Z -- applied by epic 2 (AD-26 amended with the owner's marker; condition proven on the real viewer; measured rule won, U-44)

- U-45 | veto | Story 10.1: Ruled (veto): a rerun of install.sh on an OcuTalon that is already running and healthy now prints the address and sign-in details at once and runs no docker compose up and no build. The plan had the script always run docker compose up -d, so that a rerun after an update would rebuild in place. On the IRIS runner (x64, Docker Engine) that second up -d rebuilt the image and recreated the healthy container (CI run 40021886810, measured), so the rerun waited again and a running OcuTalon was restarted, against the criterion 'prints the address and sign-in details at once when OcuTalon is already healthy' and 'never removes a container'. Any other state (stopped, failed part-way, starting, no container) still runs docker compose up -d and waits. Updating an existing install goes through the README (Story 10.2: docker compose up -d --build), not a rerun of install.sh. -> keep (by Contest Planner, 2026-10-23T22:17:18Z#134; quoting the owner: "I have delegated authority to the Contest Planner. - U-72 | veto | Story 4.1: Ruled (veto): the documented terminal command do ##class(OcuTalon.Core.Admin).SetDemoMode(0|1) checks the OcuTalon admin role strictly, as AD-19's Rule reads ('requires the OcuTalon admin role'): a terminal whose roles hold %All but not OcuTalonAdmin is refused. Measured on a throwaway: the default terminal (iris session as irisowner, Docker) holds only %All, and the README account ocutalon (OcuTalonAdmin) cannot type a programmer-mode command because no OcuTalon role holds %Development (AD-24). So on a default install nobody can run the command until a user is given both OcuTalonAdmin and terminal access. Accepting %All too would amend AD-19, which other in-flight epics' specs cite (3.1, 3.2, 10.1, 13.1), so the run may not do it on its own (doctrine U7(c), U14). -> reverse, narrowly. The terminal command SetDemoMode accepts a caller holding OcuTalonAdmin OR %All; everything else is refused as now. AD-19 is amended for the terminal entry only, with an AMENDED 2026-10-26 marker citing this item (the Contest Planner, under the owner's delegation, as U-70); the web endpoints stay OcuTalonAdmin only. Why: %All can grant itself any role, so refusing it protects nothing, while strictness makes the README's documented command fail on a default install (the terminal holds only %All, and the README account has no terminal), which is a first-run failure. The audit entry still names the person (U-74's ActingUser), and T5 still refuses when the audit entry can't be written. Tests: %All accepted, OcuTalonAdmin accepted, neither refused. Story 4.1 is done on Epic 4's branch, so make the change in Epic 4's next story or its burn-down, before Epic 4's contract. In-flight specs that cite AD-19 (3.1, 3.2, 10.1, 13.1) cite its web and role rules, which don't change. - U-75 | veto | Story 3.8: Ruled (veto) at Story 3.8's spec gate, Epic 3 (OCT-epic3). (1) Security posture: with the online-demo flag on, the Users step installs an instance-wide password guard: it generates a %SYS routine (Check^OcuTalonPasswordGuard) holding the public accounts' lower-cased names and salted SHA-512 hashes of their published passwords (never a password in clear), compiles and self-checks it, then points the instance's password-validation setting at it, before writing the public accounts' passwords; so each public account can only take its published password (measured on build 154: IRIS calls the routine for user create, an admin change and a sign-in password change; a broken routine would block every password change, hence the self-check). With the flag off it clears the setting first and removes the routine; it never replaces or removes a guard that is not OcuTalon's. (2) A malformed online-demo start value (for example a non-numeric cap) now fails the Public step at start, naming the variable, never the value. (3) New admin-only error and warning texts for these cases (no UX text). -> keep, all three parts. The guard is defence in depth behind the demo host's password filter, applies only with the online-demo flag on, keeps no password in clear, and self-checks before it is switched on. - U-103 | parked | AD-13 (capacity text): Story 4.7's measurement for DW-146 (lane-B throwaway, build 154) contradicts AD-13's session-lifetime text and AD-25's Capacity note (S13: 'each trace window holds one more connection for about two minutes after its last use, so about 3 visitors can have Visual Trace open together'). Measured: /ocutalon sign-in takes no licence unit; the first Visual Trace tab of each distinct user and client address takes one, more tabs of the same user take none; closing the tabs, or the whole browser context, frees nothing; the unit is held until sign-out (?IRISLogout=end frees it at once) or the session timeout (8 hours locally, 120 s on the online demo); at 8 units trace pages answer 503 and iris session terminals are refused. Amending AD-13/AD-25 is a re-architecture across epics (open Epic 16 specs 16.3, 16.5 and 16.6 cite AD-13; Story 16.4 'Leaving the page frees its place' and Stories 29.3/29.4 build on it) and may touch the P12/NFR-23 capacity numbers, so it is yours. Story 4.7 goes on without it: its tests sign trace tabs out (signOutTrace helper) and check licence use relatively. -> (a), as recommended: amend AD-13 and AD-25's Capacity note now with the measured facts, labelled 'measured locally on build 154 (Story 4.7, DW-146)', and the online-demo consequences labelled (inference); P12/NFR-23's numbers stay until Story 29.4 measures on the demo configuration. Route DW bullets to 16.4 (closing a tab frees nothing, so leaving must sign the trace session out with ?IRISLogout=end, with the 120 s timeout as the backstop) and 29.4 (measure units per visitor on the demo configuration). Add one more DW bullet to 29.3: at 8 units, iris session terminals are refused, so the host's own admin and reset path must not need a licence unit, or the host must keep one free. - U-125 | stopped | Story 7.1: Epic 7's Story 7.1 (repair-and-resend's dry run) needs two additions to the architecture spine before it can be built. (1) AD-16's Rule says 'The Fix area's messages are OcuTalon.Fix.Msg.Execute (ApprovalRef) and OcuTalon.Fix.Msg.FixResult (...)'. Story 7.1 needs IncidentManager to ask FixExecutor for a dry run (FixExecutor holds the one New $NAMESPACE method that may read the target production, AD-3), and Story 7.3 forbids adding a property to Execute or FixResult. So 7.1 adds two typed messages used only inside the Fix area: Fix.Msg.DryRun (IncidentRef, FixKind, Parameters) and Fix.Msg.DryRunResult (IncidentRef, Targets, DryRun, Reason). They are new stored message bodies; nothing existing changes. AD-16 is cited by finished specs (5.5) and by Epic 6's in-flight specs (6.2, 6.4), so the doctrine's U7(c) counts any amendment as a re-architecture, which the run may not make on its own (precedents U-15, U-41, U-70, U-120). (2) The spine's dependency-direction diagram lists the bridge APIs host code may use ('Bridge shared: Msg, Approval, Context, Budget, ...'). The fix kind's parameter check must run inside the bridge's own reply validation (AD-8: the bridge alone decides 'invalid'), and it needs the fields of the evidence sample the model saw. 7.1 adds a bridge class, OcuTalon.Bridge.ReplyCheck, a process-private context (Begin, End, Active, Value) that Decide begins before checking the reply and ends on every exit; the diagnosis payload's validation reads it and fails closed when it is not active. Host code reading a bridge API is the allowed direction, and the bridge still references no other OcuTalon package (AD-2). Story 7.1 waits; Epic 7 has nothing independent before it. -> (a) both: Epic 7's runner extends AD-16's Fix-messages bullet with DryRun and DryRunResult, and adds ReplyCheck to the 'Bridge shared' node, each with the marker [AMENDED 2026-10-27, Story 7.1, Epic 7 runner, orchestrator, owner U-125]; the two new message tables are accepted. Condition on ReplyCheck: Begin clears any earlier value and End runs on every exit, errors included, so a context left by one decision can never validate another decision's reply in the same job; a test pins both (the error path, and a second decision after the first). (by Contest Planner, 2026-10-27T22:08:24Z#378; quoting the owner: "I have delegated authority to the Contest Planner. That agent is allowed to make decision on my behalf."); resolved by orchestrator, 2026-10-28T04:51:05Z -- epic 7: applied in 0256361 (AD-16 DryRun/DryRunResult, ReplyCheck in Bridge shared, the ReplyCheck condition as 7.1 AC 6 with tests)

AD-22 says that for a turn case EvalIn sends EvalSink a TurnResult 'after putting the case's recorded check results in the request's HostData, which check_status reads for owner eval'. EvalIn records the case's check rows in its TurnResult before it sends the turn; check_status's evaluation branch (owner eval only, reached from WatchTools.CheckStatus) finds that turn's rows by the call's ItemId and puts them in the call's HostData, the same place the live check_status puts live results (spine line 775); the model still has to call check_status, and every other owner's path is unchanged. AD-22's spine text is not edited during the run (merged and open specs cite AD-22, so an amendment is a re-architecture under U7); its turn-case sentence and its cross-area row ('Story 9.3', now Story 9.7 after the split) are for an attended spine update. Keep? -> keep: Story 9.7 meets AD-22's intent within AD-6, with no bridge change. EvalIn records the case's checks in its TurnResult; check_status's evaluation branch (owner eval only) finds them by the turn's ItemId and puts them where the live check_status puts live results; the model still has to call the tool, and other owners are unchanged. AD-22's HostData sentence is listed for an attended spine update after the run. The alternative would wait for Epic 7, change the bridge, and put 9.7 on Epic 11's critical path. - U-173 | veto | Story 31.1: Story 31.1 (WebSocket beside polling): while the socket is open, does polling keep running? Measured in the plan: Playwright's page.route cannot intercept a WebSocket, and the existing browser specs (live, chat, decision, floor/guide) steer event delivery through routed polls; pausing polling while the socket is open would also break the reconnecting-banner specs (contended with Epic 29). Ruled: polling keeps running unchanged beside the socket (contact and the banners stay polling's; the socket only delivers sooner, through the same idempotent apply path), and those specs block the socket for their tests with one shared helper (page.routeWebSocket), their assertions unchanged; no contended file is edited. -> Accepted. Polling keeps running unchanged beside the socket; the socket only delivers sooner, through the same idempotent apply path; one shared page.routeWebSocket helper blocks the socket in the existing specs, assertions unchanged. Condition: add a test where one event arrives over both the socket and a poll and is applied exactly once (by seq). Record in the story's dev notes that polling stays on beside the socket, against the user story's wording 'polling only as the fallback'. The user-facing promise (updates arrive at once) holds, and on a single-user local install the extra polls cost nothing that matters. - U-174 | veto | Story 31.1: Story 31.1 (WebSocket beside polling): how does 'serves only a signed-in session' hold after the socket is open? Measured on build 154U: the built-in web server answers every upgrade with 101 before IRIS code runs, and a sign-out is not visible to an open socket's own CSP session for at least 10 s. Ruled (orchestrator): (1) OcuTalon.Web.Live.OnPreServer checks the role, the online-demo flag (the server refuses too, not only the page), Origin and the session before Server(), and a refusal sends no frame; (2) the page closes its socket in LiveState.stop(), which a 401, Sign out, the idle cap and an epoch change all reach, so the page's socket closes within about one poll after a sign-out; (3) the server ends every socket after a fixed 60-second lifetime and the page reconnects silently, so every reconnect re-runs the sign-in, role and Origin checks. Known cost: a client that ignores the page's close can still read its own session's events for up to 60 s after signing out. No change to how sign-in works or to the Origin check. Conditions: a test for each refusal (no session, missing role, wrong or missing Origin, online-demo flag) proving no frame is sent; a test that the page's socket closes within one poll after sign-out; a test that the server closes at the lifetime and the page reconnects with the checks re-run; no session id, cookie or token in any frame or log; and a ledger entry for a server-side sign-out check (a mark the sign-out sets, read on each socket loop) once Epic 29 has merged and Data/Session.cls is free. -> Accepted with your conditions and one change. U-175 makes 31.1 wait for Epic 29's merge, so Data/Session.cls will be free when 31.1 runs. Build the server-side sign-out check (a mark the sign-out sets, read on each socket loop) in 31.1 itself, with a test that an open socket sends no further frame within one loop after sign-out, and keep the 60 s lifetime as a backstop. That removes the known 60 s cost instead of ledgering it. Only if another open epic holds Data/Session.cls when 31.1 resumes: keep the ledger entry instead, due before 1.0.0, not open-ended. Keep the server's own online-demo refusal: the demo gate passes WebSocket upgrades (AD-24), so on the public host the server's refusal is the real guard. I will check it on the host at the 1.0.0 update. (by Contest Planner via inbox, 2026-10-30T22:43:15Z#527; quoting the owner: "I have delegated authority to the Contest Planner. That agent is allowed to make decision on my behalf."); resolved by orchestrator, 2026-10-30T22:45:56Z -- Epic 31 applied it to Story 31.1's spec (0ede48d7); built at 31.1's re-plan after Epic 29 merges

- U-176 | veto | Story 15.2: An IPM install cannot run OcuTalon's installer steps inside IPM's open transaction (measured on build 154 with IPM 0.10.10: the production cannot start there, <Ens>ErrCanNotAcquireRuntimeLock). Story 15.2 therefore commits IPM's transaction at Configure, After, then runs the steps. A failed Reload, Validate or Compile is still rolled back by IPM; a failed step leaves the new code installed and the same command runs the steps again, as on the Docker path. Story 15.3's 'if the load fails part-way, IPM rolls it back' is read as the load phases. Keep this reading? -> Keep. Commit IPM's transaction at Configure/After, then run the installer steps; the documented install stays two commands. Conditions: (1) a step that fails after the commit makes the install command report failure (never IPM's success line alone) and prints one terminal line in the Install step style naming the step, saying the new code is installed and to run the same command again; (2) a test in the FR-57 job forces one step to fail on a reinstall, through a test-only seam, not a switch in the shipped package, and proves: the command reports the failure, no data is lost, the namespace and databases stay, and a re-run completes and passes the checks of Stories 15.1 to 15.3; a failed Compile still rolls back with the previous version running; (3) docs/install-ipm.md says the same in one line; (4) mark Story 15.3's criterion in epics.md with this reading: 'the load' means IPM's Reload, Validate and Compile phases; an installer step that fails after them is recovered by running the same command again, as on the Docker path. - U-181 | veto | Epic 29: Public-text breach in pushed history. OCT-epic29's commit 3f5b3cbe (00:20Z, pushed, so public) adds one line to Epic 29's own cycle log that records the runner's pre-push check as a list of scan categories, and that list spells out both banned names (the two the public-text rule names). It is not a secret and implies no affiliation; the feature and main histories are clean, and no other branch has it. The run can't rewrite pushed history (U1), so the orchestrator ruled fix forward: the runner rewrites that line as banned_names in its next bookkeeping commit, and every runner now writes banned_names instead of the names. The old line stays in 3f5b3cbe, and joins the feature branch's history when Epic 29 merges (a --no-ff merge). Keep this? -> Keep fix forward. Low severity: a scan-category label with no description and no secret, on a non-default branch; GitHub code search indexes only the default branch's files; since the flip the repo has no forks or stars and only the owner's own visits. A rewrite now would change 16 launch-path commits and every sha quoted after 3f5b3cbe, and need a CI re-run. Additions: (1) the runner's fix-forward commit replaces the label with banned_names in that same line; the Contest Planner checks it on origin. (2) The Contest Planner scans OCT-epic30's first push after its local rewrite. (3) The owner has been told and may still ask for the scrub until Epic 29 merges. If so, the Contest Planner sends a merge hold for Epic 29 before the merge and the scrub is done attended; without that message, merge as planned. (4) Recommended, the orchestrator's call: a local pre-push guard in .git/hooks (not committed) that refuses a push whose added lines contain either banned name, so the check is mechanical, not a reading. The interval defaults to 30 min and can't be set below 30. Run-now on the demo stays (an explicit request, at most every 2 min, inside the daily cap). Tests pin that no model request is made on a tick that isn't a due beat, and none on an unchanged snapshot. PERSONA TONE (Q5): Starship computer, Pirate and Kind friend may bend the 'How you write' rules: greetings, exclamation marks and emoji are allowed. Professional keeps them strictly, and so does Another language in its language. A custom persona follows the person's own text, so it may bend them. Under every persona, facts, counts, outcomes, confidence, approvals and every catalog or reserved sentence stay exact. Make sure the AD-28 guard and the text filter don't strip emoji from persona text. Evaluation: for each fun preset, the outcomes and facts are unchanged, and the notice frames stay exact. Q7 the demo heartbeat is off until the visitor turns it on, with a sample checklist, the 30-min floor, and run-now at most every 2 min. Q8 keep AD-8 with the 4-hour back-off (yes). Q9 core memory is pins only. Q10 AD-31 is decided at the re-open. Q11 forget scope as drafted (yes). AD-21 is approved by the Contest Planner. Write it all in one planning commit. The policy pairs go into the next hold after hold9-apply.py: send them to the Contest Planner as a script with a sha guard and a dry run, like hold9-apply.py. - U-232 | veto | Story 15.3: The README's two-command IPM install (zpm "install ocutalon" in USER, then the same in OCUTALON) fails at the second run with 'No repositories are configured and enabled in this namespace', even after enable -community, because Story 15.1's MapAndStop maps only IPM's code into OCUTALON. Ruled (a): MapAndStop also maps IPM's repository definitions into OCUTALON (IPM's own 'enable -map -repos' form, or the equivalent ^IPM.Repo.Definition* mapping), so OCUTALON uses the repositories configured in the first namespace. Remote repository definitions can hold a username, password or token: they are mapped as they are, never copied, created or printed, and the guide says OCUTALON uses the first namespace's repositories. The spine's Conventions row 'IPM early step' is updated to match. -> Keep (a): map IPM's repository definitions into OCUTALON as they are, never copied or printed, and the README keeps its two commands. I checked the exposure: no built-in tool reads globals or runs SQL (the generated tool table), so the model cannot reach those definitions, and the production's code already runs with %All. Three conditions. (1) Removing or uninstalling OcuTalon removes the mapping only. It never kills or edits the first namespace's repository data, and a test asserts that the definitions are unchanged after uninstall. (2) The guide says in plain words that OCUTALON shares the first namespace's repositories, so a repository added or removed in either namespace changes both. Ruling: the runner's design stands. The retry runs in a fresh AI session seeded with Compaction.Seed of the conversation, and the retry runner never links it. The retry skips the Held check only for the request that names the first runner in RetryOf. After a completed, untainted Chat it points the first runner's link at its session. ConversationLink.Point is a compare-and-set under the link row's own lock, never the conversation lock, so a late first attempt can never move the link off the retry. Condition: AD-6 gets an additive sub-bullet that names this as the retry of the same request, not a new turn. The rule that a new turn waits while an earlier one is still running stays word for word. A test pins both: a request with RetryOf skips Held, and a request without it still waits. The marker reads [AMENDED 2026-11-03, Story 33.1, orchestrator unattended ruling]. Also allowed: the Saved conversations clause plus a new 'Retried turns' convention row (the AC orders the convention row amended); S1, which adds Time to the bridge shared node label plus the permitted edges Checks->Digest.Data.CheckRun, Checks->Schedule.Heartbeat.Gaps (merged on feature) and Digest->Checks.Coverage.Build and Checks.Render; and lint_spine ok. No other in-flight epic's own specs cite AD-6. Only merged stories do, and their behaviour for ordinary turns is unchanged. -> Keep, with one added condition. RetryOf is set only by the server's AD-8 second-model retry path. No client-facing entry (web API, Telegram, mail, bridge, MCP) can carry or set it, so the Held skip cannot be used to jump the conversation lock. The retry must name a runner of the same conversation and the same request (ItemId), or it waits like any new turn. Add a test that a request arriving through the web API with a RetryOf value still waits (or the field is ignored). Everything else as ruled: fresh session seeded by Compaction.Seed, compare-and-set under the link row's own lock, the additive AD-6 sub-bullet with the new-turn wait rule unchanged word for word, both cases pinned, the marker and the convention row. - U-247 | veto | Story 33.1 (setting storage: the instance time zone's run-time source): Task 0 measured run-time zone change on build 154: $SYSTEM.Process.TimeZone converts 12 instants around the 2026 DST changes correctly. A running job picked up a change in 0.07-0.36 s and a running business host in 0.25-1.16 s, with no restart, so the AC's process-zone-plus-restart fallback is not used. Ruling: the instance zone's single run-time source is Decide's host setting InstanceZone (category Bridge, an IANA name; empty means the container's TZ). It is read only through OcuTalon.Bridge.Time.Zone() and written only through Bridge.Time.SetZone(name), which rejects unknown names because an unknown name silently converts as UTC. Bridge.Time.Apply() re-applies Process.TimeZone only when the name has changed. This follows AD-19, which already keeps bridge values in host settings. A new Bridge.Data table was rejected because it would also edit Test/Data/Tables.cls, which Epic 39 edits. Side finding DW-643 (Data.Time.FromLocal resolves the repeated fall-back hour differently depending on the preceding call) is routed to Story 18.2. -> Keep. InstanceZone as a Decide host setting (AD-19), read only through Bridge.Time.Zone() and written only through SetZone(name), which rejects unknown IANA names; Apply() re-applies only on change; no restart fallback, per Task 0's measurements on build 154. Rejecting a new Bridge.Data table to stay out of Epic 39's Test/Data/Tables.cls is right. DW-643 to Story 18.2 is fine. The settings-page write in 17.4 stays audited under FR-17. AD-3 is cited by 77 specs incl. done stories, so under doctrine U7 this is a re-architecture the orchestrator may not rule. Also veto items V1-V12 (V2 enables IRIS's unauthenticated /csp/sys/oauth2 while a mail OAuth2 client is registered; never on the online demo) and new PUBLIC TEXT N1-N5. Approve S1 + V1-V12 + N1-N5? -> A, with conditions. S1 approved as an AD-3 extension, marked [AMENDED 2026-11-12, Story 21.3, owner delegation via the Contest Planner]; V1-V13 approved; N1-N4 approved as drafted; N5 edited. (1) S1 scope: Sys does only what S1 lists (create, reuse or delete OcuTalonMail and its server definition; set the /csp/sys/oauth2 enabled flag under V2; read the token's signed-in state). Any other %SYS read or write is a review HIGH. Sys also refuses on its own, outside SettingsOps' job and on the online demo, and a test proves each refusal. (2) V2: OcuTalon enables /csp/sys/oauth2 only during an admin's register or sign-in action, never at startup or in the background, and never re-enables it after an admin disabled it except on the next explicit sign-in. Each enable and disable is audited (V10); email.oauthOwned records whether OcuTalon enabled it, and removal acts on that as drafted. (3) The model and every tool path can never reach MailSignIn or Sys: origins web_admin only, plus an AD-10 test that a tainted or agent turn cannot submit MailSignIn. (4) V6/V11 follow the same rule as 21.6 (c): the provider endpoints are https constants with the host-name check, plain HTTP to the mock exists only behind OCUTALON_TEST_OAUTH_MOCK=1, a test proves the shipped compose files and the image never set it, and the switch logs a warning when it is on. (5) The scopes are the minimum for POP3 and SMTP (Google https://mail.google.com/; Microsoft POP and SMTP.Send plus offline_access), constants listed in the spec. Task 0 records whether the IRIS client supports PKCE: use it if it does; if not, note it, with no blocker. (6) N5 replaced by: "- On an install where an admin signs a mailbox in with Google or Microsoft 365, IRIS's OAuth2 redirection endpoint `/csp/sys/oauth2` is turned on for as long as that sign-in is configured, and turned off again when it is removed, unless another OAuth2 client still needs it. The client secret is kept only in IRIS's OAuth2 client, and the tokens only in IRIS's OAuth2 token store. None of this runs on the online demo." (7) The demo host check (Planner) adds: /csp/sys/oauth2 is disabled on the demo. Not 1.0.8-critical: it merges when it is green.
